I haven't lost a ton of weight or anything, I just... I'm just trying to keep it from getting out of hand after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: When these eating episodes happen, do you feel unable to stop eating or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's... well, once I start on those nights, it's almost like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'll be in my head telling myself to just put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. I really can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do your body shape or weight strongly affect how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I definitely don't want to gain weight, which is why I do what I do after I eat. But I don't know if I'd say my weight or shape completely dictates my self-worth or how I feel about myself overall. I just... I just don't want to get heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do these binge-and-vomit episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since my recent apartment move kind of settled into a routine... I'd say most weeks I have at least one of those late-night episodes. I'll eat way past what I meant to, and then I try to make up for it. Usually I'll throw up, or... sometimes I'll just skip breakfast the next morning, or force myself to go on a really long run to burn it off.\n\nDoctor: Have these binge-and-vomit episodes been happening like this for at least the past 3 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been going on for about that long. Like I said, ever since I got settled into the new apartment, it's been pretty consistent. So, yeah, definitely for the last three months, maybe a little longer, I've been having those episodes at least once a week where I just eat too much and then have to fix it by throwing up or running or fasting.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes, do you eat an unusually large amount of food within a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Usually it's after my late shifts at work. I'll stop for food on the way back, and then I just... keep going once I get home. I'll have a full takeout meal, and then I'll move on to chips, sweets, really whatever's in the apartment. I'm just trying to keep it from getting out of hand after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: When these eating episodes happen, do you feel unable to stop eating or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's... well, once I start on those nights, it's almost like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'll be in my head telling myself to just put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. I really can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do your body shape or weight strongly affect how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I definitely don't want to gain weight, which is why I do what I do after I eat. But I don't know if I'd say my weight or shape completely dictates my self-worth or how I feel about myself overall. I just... I just don't want to get heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do these binge-and-vomit episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since my recent apartment move kind of settled into a routine... I'd say most weeks I have at least one of those late-night episodes. I'll eat way past what I meant to, and then I try to make up for it. Usually I'll throw up, or... sometimes I'll just skip breakfast the next morning, or force myself to go on a really long run to burn it off.\n\nDoctor: Have these binge-and-vomit episodes been happening like this for at least the past 3 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been going on for about that long. Like I said, ever since I got settled into the new apartment, it's been pretty consistent. So, yeah, definitely for the last three months, maybe a little longer, I've been having those episodes at least once a week where I just eat too much and then have to fix it by throwing up or running or fasting.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes, do you eat an unusually large amount of food within a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Usually it's after my late shifts at work. I'll stop for food on the way back, and then I just... keep going once I get home. I'll have a full takeout meal, and then I'll move on to chips, sweets, really whatever's in the apartment. It all happens pretty fast, and afterward, I'm honestly just shocked by how much I actually ate.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your body shape or weight has a big impact on how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, honestly. I mean, I want to stay in shape, sure, but I wouldn't say my weight or how I look is the main thing I care about when it comes to who I am. It's more just... I hate the feeling of losing control with the food, you know? But my body shape isn't really the big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Not a lot, no. I mean, it's frustrating, like I said, because I don't like losing control of what I'm eating. But I wouldn't say I'm deeply distressed by it or anything. It's more just... an annoying cycle I've gotten stuck in, I guess. I do it, I fix it, and then I just go about my week.\n\nDoctor: After these overeating episodes, do you usually make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like I said, after those late shifts when I eat all that takeout and whatever else is around, I'll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just don't want all that food sitting in my stomach and turning into extra weight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But I don't know if I'd say my weight or shape completely dictates my self-worth or how I feel about myself overall. I just... I just don't want to get heavy.\n\nDoctor: How often do these binge-and-vomit episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since my recent apartment move kind of settled into a routine... I'd say most weeks I have at least one of those late-night episodes. I'll eat way past what I meant to, and then I try to make up for it. Usually I'll throw up, or... sometimes I'll just skip breakfast the next morning, or force myself to go on a really long run to burn it off.\n\nDoctor: Have these binge-and-vomit episodes been happening like this for at least the past 3 months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been going on for about that long. Like I said, ever since I got settled into the new apartment, it's been pretty consistent. So, yeah, definitely for the last three months, maybe a little longer, I've been having those episodes at least once a week where I just eat too much and then have to fix it by throwing up or running or fasting.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes, do you eat an unusually large amount of food within a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Usually it's after my late shifts at work. I'll stop for food on the way back, and then I just... keep going once I get home. I'll have a full takeout meal, and then I'll move on to chips, sweets, really whatever's in the apartment. It all happens pretty fast, and afterward, I'm honestly just shocked by how much I actually ate.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your body shape or weight has a big impact on how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, honestly. I mean, I want to stay in shape, sure, but I wouldn't say my weight or how I look is the main thing I care about when it comes to who I am. It's more just... I hate the feeling of losing control with the food, you know? But my body shape isn't really the big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Not a lot, no. I mean, it's frustrating, like I said, because I don't like losing control of what I'm eating. But I wouldn't say I'm deeply distressed by it or anything. It's more just... an annoying cycle I've gotten stuck in, I guess. I do it, I fix it, and then I just go about my week.\n\nDoctor: After these overeating episodes, do you usually make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like I said, after those late shifts when I eat all that takeout and whatever else is around, I'll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just don't want all that food sitting in my stomach and turning into extra weight.\n\nDoctor: After these eating episodes, do you do anything to prevent weight gain besides vomiting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. After a bad night of eating, I’ll usually make myself run extra miles on the trail the next morning. Or I'll just skip breakfast before my shift starts. Even when I’m really tired from the night before, it just feels like I have to do it to... you know, undo it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'll eat a full takeout meal, and then I'll just start grabbing whatever else is around—bags of chips, sweets, just anything I can find. And then when I finally stop, I look around and I'm just kind of shocked by how much I actually ate.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I haven't lost a ton of weight or anything, I just... I'm just trying to keep it from getting out of hand after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: When these eating episodes happen, do you feel unable to stop eating or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's... well, once I start on those nights, it's almost like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'll be in my head telling myself to just put the food away, but my hands just keep opening containers until everything is gone. I really can't seem to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Do your body shape or weight strongly affect how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I definitely don't want to gain weight, which is why I do what I do after I eat. I'd say most weeks I have at least one of those late-night episodes. I'll eat way past what I meant to, and then I try to make up for it. Like I said, ever since I got settled into the new apartment, it's been pretty consistent. Usually it's after my late shifts at work. I'll stop for food on the way back, and then I just... keep going once I get home. I'll have a full takeout meal, and then I'll move on to chips, sweets, really whatever's in the apartment. It all happens pretty fast, and afterward, I'm honestly just shocked by how much I actually ate.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your body shape or weight has a big impact on how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, honestly. I mean, I want to stay in shape, sure, but I wouldn't say my weight or how I look is the main thing I care about when it comes to who I am. It's more just... I hate the feeling of losing control with the food, you know? But my body shape isn't really the big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Not a lot, no. I mean, it's frustrating, like I said, because I don't like losing control of what I'm eating. But I wouldn't say I'm deeply distressed by it or anything. It's more just... an annoying cycle I've gotten stuck in, I guess. I do it, I fix it, and then I just go about my week.\n\nDoctor: After these overeating episodes, do you usually make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like I said, after those late shifts when I eat all that takeout and whatever else is around, I'll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just don't want all that food sitting in my stomach and turning into extra weight.\n\nDoctor: After these eating episodes, do you do anything to prevent weight gain besides vomiting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. After a bad night of eating, I’ll usually make myself run extra miles on the trail the next morning. Or I'll just skip breakfast before my shift starts. Even when I’m really tired from the night before, it just feels like I have to do it to... you know, undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes feel very upsetting or distressing to you?\n\nPatient: Uh, I think you might have asked me that a couple of minutes ago. But, yeah, like I said, not really. It's definitely frustrating, but I wouldn't call it deeply upsetting or distressing. It's just... an annoying habit I've gotten into, and then I deal with it and move on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, fast, or exercise hard even on days when you have not had one of those overeating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I try to stay somewhat active because of my job, but I don't go out of my way to throw up or skip meals or run myself into the ground if I haven't had one of those bad nights with food. It's really just tied to when I eat too much.\n\nDoctor: When these binge-eating episodes happen, do they cause you marked distress?\n\nPatient: I think we just covered this, but... no, they don't. Like I was just saying, I wouldn't call it distress, marked or otherwise. It's definitely frustrating when I do it, sure, but I just deal with it by running or skipping a meal and then I go to work. It's not something I'm agonizing over.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight or shape play a major role in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I want to look decent, especially since I'm on my feet and around people a lot at work, but it's not like my whole self-worth is tied up in my weight or how I look. I judge myself more on, you know, if I'm doing a good job and showing up when I'm supposed to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So, yeah, definitely for the last three months, maybe a little longer, I've been having those episodes at least once a week where I just eat too much and then have to fix it by throwing up or running or fasting.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes, do you eat an unusually large amount of food within a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Usually it's after my late shifts at work. I'll stop for food on the way back, and then I just... keep going once I get home. I'll have a full takeout meal, and then I'll move on to chips, sweets, really whatever's in the apartment. It all happens pretty fast, and afterward, I'm honestly just shocked by how much I actually ate.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your body shape or weight has a big impact on how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, honestly. I mean, I want to stay in shape, sure, but I wouldn't say my weight or how I look is the main thing I care about when it comes to who I am. It's more just... I hate the feeling of losing control with the food, you know? But my body shape isn't really the big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Not a lot, no. I mean, it's frustrating, like I said, because I don't like losing control of what I'm eating. But I wouldn't say I'm deeply distressed by it or anything. It's more just... an annoying cycle I've gotten stuck in, I guess. I do it, I fix it, and then I just go about my week.\n\nDoctor: After these overeating episodes, do you usually make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Like I said, after those late shifts when I eat all that takeout and whatever else is around, I'll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just don't want all that food sitting in my stomach and turning into extra weight.\n\nDoctor: After these eating episodes, do you do anything to prevent weight gain besides vomiting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. After a bad night of eating, I’ll usually make myself run extra miles on the trail the next morning. Or I'll just skip breakfast before my shift starts. Even when I’m really tired from the night before, it just feels like I have to do it to... you know, undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge-eating episodes feel very upsetting or distressing to you?\n\nPatient: Uh, I think you might have asked me that a couple of minutes ago. But, yeah, like I said, not really. It's definitely frustrating, but I wouldn't call it deeply upsetting or distressing. It's just... an annoying habit I've gotten into, and then I deal with it and move on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, fast, or exercise hard even on days when you have not had one of those overeating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I try to stay somewhat active because of my job, but I don't go out of my way to throw up or skip meals or run myself into the ground if I haven't had one of those bad nights with food. It's really just tied to when I eat too much.\n\nDoctor: When these binge-eating episodes happen, do they cause you marked distress?\n\nPatient: I think we just covered this, but... no, they don't. Like I was just saying, I wouldn't call it distress, marked or otherwise. It's definitely frustrating when I do it, sure, but I just deal with it by running or skipping a meal and then I go to work. It's not something I'm agonizing over.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight or shape play a major role in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I want to look decent, especially since I'm on my feet and around people a lot at work, but it's not like my whole self-worth is tied up in my weight or how I look. I judge myself more on, you know, if I'm doing a good job and showing up when I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly do something to prevent weight gain after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do.
